- **South Georgia and the South Sandwich Islands**: REMA (PGC) covers "the subantarctic islands near South America"; CC-BY-4.0; South Georgia: four 2 m mosaic tiles (dates 2008-12-15 to 2012-04-02), South Sandwich ten tiles (data_perc 0-0.084) [https://stac.pgc.umn.edu/api/v1/collections/rema-mosaics-v2.0-2m] [https://stac.pgc.umn.edu/api/v1/collections/rema-mosaics-v2.0-2m/items?bbox=-38.3,-55.0,-35.6,-53.9&limit=12]. The South Georgia GIS (BAS Mapping and Geographic Information Centre) serves openly: "SG DEM" 20 m grid (8040 x 5374 cells; "ASTER GDEM, SRTM data, five DEMs created from VHR imagery, and one DEM created from aerial photography. Creation date: 2017", glacier caution) and a 5 m "Shackleton Crossing DEM" (two WorldView-1 stereo pairs 2013-14, "No ground control points were used"); WMS "Fees none", "AccessConstraints none" [https://www.sggis.gov.gs/geoserver/ows?service=WMS&request=GetCapabilities&version=1.3.0] [https://www.sggis.gov.gs/geoserver/ows?service=WCS&request=GetCapabilities&version=2.0.1] [https://www.sggis.gov.gs/geoserver/ows?service=WCS&request=DescribeCoverage&version=2.0.1&coverageId=sggis__sg_crossing_dem]. GSGSSI (Wayback): SGGIS "comprehensively updated in 2016-17 ... high-resolution mapping data for key areas", "There is no login requirement" [https://web.archive.org/web/20261002195405/https://gov.gs/gis/]. No lidar found. BAS drone DSMs for seal colonies exist (research output, OGL v3.0) [https://data-search.nerc.ac.uk/geonetwork/srv/api/records/303d8002-712c-4979-b31b-54e421100def/formatters/xml].
- **Clipperton**: Shom/PASSION coastal topo-bathymetric MNT of Ile de la Passion, 20 m grid (and a 100 m facade grid), data "acquises entre 2012 et 2015", heights above the lowest astronomical tide, Licence Ouverte 2.0, "Ne pas utiliser pour la navigation" [https://services.data.shom.fr/geonetwork/srv/api/records/MNT_COTIER_PASSION_CLIPPERTON_20m_WGS84.xml/formatters/xml]. CORRECTION: the download URL printed in the record is 404; the INSPIRE prepackage form works: 9,142,726 bytes, anonymous [https://services.data.shom.fr/INSPIRE/telechargement/prepackageGroup/MNT_COTIER_CLIPPERTON_PACK_DL]. Only marginally finer than GLO-30 [https://copernicus-dem-30m.s3.amazonaws.com/Copernicus_DSM_COG_10_N10_00_W110_00_DEM/Copernicus_DSM_COG_10_N10_00_W110_00_DEM.tif].


## 4. Non-governmental sources (all NON-GOVERNMENTAL unless marked; first-pass agent nongov, 169 logged requests; its quotes are each under 15 words)

Bottom line: no non-governmental source beats the national or subnational data above anywhere except on small research footprints. The useful ones are re-packagers of governmental data (Mapterhorn) and research lidar patches (OpenTopography). The commercial DEMs are paid and none permits an offline cache for a personal map without a negotiated licence. "Offline" = may I download and cache or tile it for my own offline map.

| Source | Type | What / resolution / Americas area | Beats national or global? | Licence (quote) | Offline cache | Access | URL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mapterhorn (open project, PMTiles; maintainers named on its site) | open-source project, NLnet NGI0 funded; channel for governmental data | Terrarium WebP tiles, 151 source datasets. Americas non-US: Canada DTM 2 m partial (NRCan HRDEM, 526 GB tarball), Greenland 2 m (Klimadatastyrelsen "Elevation model Greenland", 641 GB, https://dataforsyningen.dk/data/4780), Saba / Sint Eustatius / Bonaire 0.5 m, Guadeloupe + Martinique RGE ALTI 1 m / 5 m, French Guiana 1 m / 5 m, Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelon 1 m / 5 m; GLO-30 elsewhere. Nothing for Mexico, Central or South America (except French Guiana), the rest of the Caribbean, Bermuda or the Falklands | Yes where listed; for Canada it only repackages the NRCan product | each source CC BY 4.0, OGL-Canada or Licence Ouverte 2.0; no separate Mapterhorn terms of use found; the site's robots.txt carries `ai-train=no` for web content and names AI crawlers, the `*` group allows everything | YES: "Download Mapterhorn data extracts for a limited area"; redistribution with the per-source credit (my reading) | anonymous HTTPS tiles `tiles.mapterhorn.com/{z}/{x}/{y}.webp` (a Bonaire z12 tile was 64,218 bytes), `pmtiles extract --bbox`; planet z0-12 = 355,579,263,191 bytes (HEAD); z13+ files e.g. Bonaire 1.1 GB, Saba/Statia 18.0 GB, Guadeloupe/Martinique 14.6 GB, French Guiana 80.6 GB | https://mapterhorn.com/ ; https://mapterhorn.com/data-access ; https://download.mapterhorn.com/attribution.json ; https://download.mapterhorn.com/download_urls.json |

### 4.1 OpenTopography datasets in the Americas outside the USA (from the DCAT dump https://portal.opentopography.org/csw/catalog.json; landing pages `https://portal.opentopography.org/datasetMetadata?otCollectionID=<OT.id>` or `.../dataspace/dataset?opentopoID=<OTDS.id>` were fetched for resolution/area where noted)

- Canada: HRDEM mosaic 1 m (1,445,766.9 km2, 2018-2025; OGL-Canada; "limited to academics") OT.062025.3979.1; MRDEM 30 m (14,105,744 km2) OT.032025.3979.1; Montreal 2015 lidar (648 km2, 15 pts/m2, CC BY 4.0; academics only) OT.082023.2950.1; Tintina Fault, Yukon drone lidar (3.99 km2, 78.42 pts/m2, CC BY-NC 4.0) OTDS.112024.3154.1; Columbia Lake drone lidar (3.54 km2, 90.23 pts/m2, CC BY 4.0) OTDS.102023.2955.1; Denali Fault, Elk Lake Fault, San Juan Fault drone lidar (CC BY 4.0, not opened).
- Mexico: Middle Usumacinta (943.32 km2, 17.91 pts/m2, 0.5 m, CC BY 4.0) OT.042022.32615.1; G-LiHT Yucatan West (585.69 km2, 3.18 pts/m2) OT.112026.32615.1 and East (572.93 km2, 4.44 pts/m2) OT.112026.32616.1, CC BY 4.0; Salsipuedes 2014 (5.6 km2), Agua Blanca Fault (76.27 km2, 6.97 pts/m2), El Mayor-Cucapah (372 km2, 9.12 pts/m2), all CC BY 4.0; Guadalupe Island SfM (21.24 km2, CC0); Tecolote volcano, Crater Elegante (Sonora), El Mayor-Cucapah TLS (no licence in DCAT; not opened).
- Central America and Caribbean: Caracol/Chiquibul, Belize (64.14 km2, 23.91 pts/m2, "Not Provided"); ten Guatemala 2024 UAV lidar patches (Gualan 5.94 km2 at 90.96 pts/m2; CC BY 4.0); La Selva, Costa Rica (736.34 km2, 0.2 pts/m2, "Not Provided"); Haiti 2010 earthquake lidar (838 km2, 3.4 pts/m2, "Not Provided" but description says "All data are available in the public domain") OT.072010.32618.1; Haiti DTM 2014-2016 (1.5 m, 29,239 km2) OT.082021.32618.1.
- South America: ANADEM 30 m DTM (17,815,056 km2, CC BY 4.0) OT.082025.4674.1; Sao Paulo 2017 lidar (1,629 km2, 19.49 pts/m2, GPLv3, academics only) OT.062020.31983.1; Garopaba dunes, Santa Catarina (10 km2, 2.13 pts/m2); Garopaba and Lagoinha TLS/SfM clouds (not opened); Chile: Santiago SPOT-derived (1,695 km2, 0.05 pts/m2, photogrammetry, CC BY 4.0), San Ramon Fault, Chinchilla scarp (CC0); Peru: Incapuquio Fault (three zones) and Purgatorio Fault (CC0); Galapagos: Sierra Negra volcano (0.84 km2 at 191.81 pts/m2, CC BY 4.0, plus three TIR flights).
- Nothing in the catalogue for Colombia, Venezuela, the Guianas, Bolivia, Paraguay, Uruguay, Argentina, Honduras, El Salvador, Nicaragua, Panama, Cuba, Jamaica, Dominican Republic, Bahamas, Greenland, Bermuda or the Falklands (bbox-centre and keyword pass over the 811 records).



## 5. Unverified (each with every route tried)

General causes, valid for most items below: many Latin American government hosts time out, reset, answer "No route to host" or fail TLS verification from this network (none was circumvented: no proxies, no User-Agent changes, no TLS bypass; two second-pass agents ran the unmodified helper with a complete Mozilla CA bundle via SSL_CERT_FILE because the macOS bundle lacks some roots, which keeps verification on); the shared web-search budget ran out part-way in the first pass; several portals are JavaScript shells. Hosts that timed out were retried at least 10 minutes later in the second pass (the retry times are in the agents' fetch logs; a few retries came sooner and are noted). HEAD requests made with the helper's `head` command are not logged, so file sizes that rest only on a HEAD say "by HEAD" in section 3.

**Canada**
- Alberta: AltaLIS airborne-lidar terms and prices (altalis.com is an Angular shell; its module manifests carry no catalogue; Wayback gives the same shell; aep.alberta.ca did not resolve; geodiscover.alberta.ca timed out). A search snippet's "4 km2 minimum order, about CAD 1,760" is not confirmed by any fetched page.
- Manitoba: coverage share of the MLI project DEMs (only the Pembina and Red River zips were HEAD-checked). Saskatchewan: coverage share of the province's lidar ImageServer; Water Security Agency / ISC lidar pages not located. Newfoundland and Labrador: which parts of Labrador the 5 m DEM covers. Nunavut: no territorial elevation programme beyond community contours. NWT: pages 2-40 of the Centre for Geomatics data list; any GNWT lidar footprint service. Yukon: ftp.geomaticsyukon.ca (FTP is not supported by the helper); the share of the territory that the image-service items cover (the 1.7 % lidar and 10.5 % stereo-DSM sums are first-pass figures, not re-computed).
- Quebec: the province's area (the "about 40 %" of the province covered by the 614,801 latest tiles is the first-pass share; the tile count itself was re-read); the separate 1 m MNT index zip (over the 5 MB cap).
- Ontario: the first-pass figures of 47 projects covering 171,888 km2 (16 % of the province) and 1.3-26 pulses/m2 cannot be reproduced from the image service as it answers now (40 projects with primary tiles, 43 names in the project list, no pulse-density field), so they stay marked as first-pass reads; the union of the provincial lidar programme and the FRI single-photon lidar was never computed. HRDEM holds 49.2 % of Ontario at 1 m (recomputed from the STAC), against 53.1 % in the first-pass footprint layer; the gap is unexplained.
- New Brunswick: tile-download mechanism (JS viewer) and vertical accuracy. Nova Scotia: whether the Elevation Explorer's per-tile downloads are governed by the "Unrestricted" licence beyond what the datalocator page links (the licence text itself and the project figures were re-read). Alberta: nothing open (the project counts were re-read: 43 Alberta-provided; 63 NRCan-provided against 62 in the first pass).
- Federal: per-project accuracy values in the HRDEM metadata geodatabase (the `.gdb.zip` returned 403 at the legacy download host); whether the 1 m mosaic holds data absent from hrdem-lidar (checked on one 2.3 MB coverage file only; the other coverage files were not compared); real valid-pixel coverage inside the ArcticDEM envelopes; province boundaries better than Natural Earth 1:50m (geoBoundaries' files are Git LFS objects not fetched, geo.statcan.gc.ca fails TLS twice 15 minutes apart, www12.statcan.gc.ca answers 403).

**Mexico**
- INEGI: CEM 4.0 year, accuracy and method; per-sheet 5 m / 1.5 m download URLs, sizes, years, pts/m2, vertical accuracy and datum (the lookup is a POST the helper cannot send; the four INEGI relieve PDFs (CEM 3.0 factsheet, MDE description, high-resolution LiDAR sheet, data sheets) were read whole and say nothing of CEM 4.0 or per-sheet years); the current LAS point-cloud portal URL.
- Federal bodies: CFE (www.cfe.mx fails certificate verification, gob.mx/cfe 404, archive refused); CENAPRED's Atlas Nacional de Riesgos (robots timeout, archive refused); CONAFOR/IDEFOR live catalogue (TLS) and the names of its 26 raster layers; CONABIO's WMS beyond its first 5 MB (the capabilities file is 63 MB, not read whole); SEDATU's state cadastre PDFs for Morelos, Nuevo Leon, San Luis Potosi, Yucatan, Zacatecas (not found under the guessed file names).
- State hosts unreachable even after a retry: Aguascalientes (three hosts answer robots.txt 403), Baja California Sur (www.bcs.gob.mx, sig.bcs.gob.mx), Ciudad de Mexico (live portal and sieg), Chihuahua (SIGECH viewer launched April 2026 per a search result, press PDF host timed out), Estado de Mexico (IGECEM live; a paid-3D-model tariff seen only in a search result), Guerrero (ide.guerrero.gob.ar SSL EOF, seed.guerrero.gob.mx), Jalisco (datos.jalisco.gob.mx DNS; lidar.guadalajara.gob.mx timed out three times), Michoacan (www.michoacan.gob.mx TLS, sigsedum 403), Durango (www.durango.gob.mx TLS), Puebla (all live hosts refused), Tabasco and Tlaxcala (timeouts), Sonora (robots 403), Nayarit (robots 403 and TLS faults), Veracruz (live site and civil-protection maps), Quintana Roo (qroo.gob.mx and IGECE hosts time out; tender PDFs have no Wayback copy), Oaxaca (SISPLADE), Yucatan (SIGEY hosts DNS), Morelos (CEIEG live), Zacatecas (COEPLA certificate chain), Nuevo Leon (nl.gob.mx robots 403; atlas REST HTTP 500). The CEIEG minutes and state programme PDFs on snieg.mx were reachable only until the host began resetting connections.
- Findings that stay open: whether the Nuevo Leon 2012 lidar is the same flight as INEGI's NL lidar; the Jalisco cadastre lidar licence and timetable; Guadalajara lidar density, licence and download links; K'usam, Acapulco and UAT data access; Tabasco's Centro lidar and Tamaulipas's UAT lidar release; the Queretaro 2016 DEM; Puerto Morelos lidar density and licence; NCALM Puuc (Campeche, 238 km2, only a CAPTCHA page).

Regional: CATHALAC site under construction; servir.sica.int and ccad.sica.int no DNS.

- Cuba: GEOCUBA, ONEI, IPF, INOTU live hosts (DNS failure / expired certificates, tried twice 38 minutes apart; Wayback read); the Cuban national IDE (no snapshot for iderc.cu, geoportal.onei.cu, geoportal.cu); closed data cannot be ruled out. Jamaica: World Bank DEM provenance (API 429); NSDMB live site (expired certificate, DNS); Mona GeoInformatics holdings. Dominica: contents, resolution and openness of the Dominode lidar (hosts 502 three times; Wayback catalogue is JavaScript). Anguilla ("Department of Lands & Surveys Website" link not followed), Montserrat, BVI (Town and Country Planning, GIS Unit; Tortola, Virgin Gorda coverage), Antigua (Lands and Surveys, DCA), Saint Kitts and Nevis (Physical Planning; release of the 2024 lidar), Saint Lucia (2009 DEM cell size; whether DPDUR releases the lidar), Barbados (lidar density; gisbarbados.gov.bb robots 403), Trinidad and Tobago (lidar density; the Division's own offer; data.gov.tt expired certificate twice; Wayback fetch refused), Grenada (government geoportal page and licence; DTM source cell size), Aruba (kadaster.aw DNS failure: the evidence is the DOW home and ArcGIS Online only), Sint Maarten (kadaster.sx Wayback copy refused), Curacao (Dienst Openbare Werken not located; kadaster.cw robots 403, Wayback read), Dominican Republic (tender terms of reference, minpre.gob.do 403; how to request the 2012 SGN data), Haiti (original HaitiData layer page and licence), Bermuda (DEM2012 cell size; whether the tile service is bare earth; its licence), Falklands (SAERI portal answered 503; whether the minefield DTMs were published). Regional: NV5/CCCCC deliverables beyond Barbados and Tobago; NOAA/USACE foreign-lidar catalogues (not queried); GFDRR OpenDRI; charim.net (self-signed certificate; Wayback chapter read).
- French and Dutch territories: IGN LiDAR HD tile files themselves (presence rests on the WFS index and WMS); Shom Litto3D per-package download URLs for Saint-Barthelemy and Saint-Martin (the shop pages require an account); the USGS St Vincent zips (rest on the ScienceBase listing); the coverage share of the three Dutch islands (publisher statement only).

Leads from search snippets only (not evidence): QFuego-Patagonia portal, a Universidad de Chile lidar DEM on Zenodo, a CC BY 4.0 claim for the Haiti DTM that contradicts OpenTopography's page, PlanetDEM.

## 6. Conduct notes

- Identification: every request used the User-Agent `scenic-roads/0.1 (personal offline map)` through the shared helper; no contact details, no personal data, no sign-ups, logins or CAPTCHAs (the PE3D CAPTCHA, the Medellin / Montevideo / gov.br / JAXA / Earthdata logins, the SIGTIERRAS login and the Dataforsyningen FTPS account were not attempted). Blocks were not circumvented: Cloudflare / WAF / bot-check pages (IBGE, igm.cl, IPP Rio, SIGGARR, catalogo.geoidep.gob.pe for wildcard queries, Tamaulipas and Veracruz civil-protection, geosinager.defensacivil.gob.bo, ipde.gob.pa, cne.go.cr), 403s (Aguascalientes, Sonora, Nuevo Leon, Jujuy, Catamarca, legacy NRCan download host), TLS faults (many) and HTTP 401 (dados.gov.br) were reported and routed around by other catalogues or Wayback only.
- Helper behaviour. First pass: page fetches were refused until robots.txt had been fetched for the host, but fetches marked `api` skipped that step for documented APIs and data endpoints, and a disallowed path was only reported, not refused. Second pass: robots.txt is fetched automatically for every host and every request (page, API, data) is refused when a Disallow matches, also for /api/ paths; hosts whose robots.txt cannot be read (403, 5xx, timeout, TLS) are refused until it can be read (stricter than RFC 9309, which allows access after a 4xx); Crawl-delay is slept (up to 60 s); redirects are followed hop by hop with the same checks; TLS errors are never bypassed; mapterhorn.com is refused. These helper rules were stricter than the owner's final rule (12 Nov): robots.txt applies to real crawling only (bulk or link-following fetches); reading a page, record, API answer or published file as a person would needs no check. None of the 12 Nov re-reads below crawled or followed links.
- Robots audit, superseded: after the second pass I checked every URL printed in this note against its host's robots.txt (RFC 9309 matching) and moved to section 5 the facts whose only evidence was a URL under a Disallow rule (193 of the 2,454 first-pass URLs with a 2xx answer, and URLs on eight hosts whose robots.txt could not be read). On 12 Nov the owner set the rule that robots.txt is for real crawling only, so that audit no longer applies and the facts it dropped were restored by re-reading each source once (User-Agent `scenic-roads/0.1 (personal offline map)`, one request every one to two seconds, nothing over 5 MB downloaded; the Redape loop made one request per dataset record, 145 in all). Restored with a fresh read: Nova Scotia (project areas, pulse density, accuracy, datums, tile counts, licence text); Ontario (0.5 m cell, tile and project counts, end years, accuracy classes, access); Quebec (tile counts, densities, datum, sheet index and sizes); Alberta (federal project counts, catalogue API, provincial DEM metadata); BC (catalogue API records); Yukon (image-service items); the federal HRDEM Project Footprints layer and the HRDEM Mosaic and CDEM specifications; Redape (145 lidar datasets with counts per state, densities, licence); Zenodo API fields (EBA, Motagua, Belham); the Panama CKAN API. Restored from the first-pass wording only, marked "read 12 Nov, first pass; not re-read": the Falklands SRTM portal record (SAERI portal answered 503 and timeouts on the re-read), Ontario's 47 projects, 171,888 km2 and 1.3-26 pulses/m2 (the service answers differently now), Quebec's "about 40 % of the province" and the Yukon footprint shares. Canada's coverage shares were recomputed in the second pass from the federal STAC and S3 listings; those recomputed figures stay as the headline (the first-pass shares from the footprint layer agree within 0.4 point except Ontario, 53.1 % against 49.2 %). Limits: only facts that this note recorded as dropped could be restored; the other first-pass reads under Disallow rules (CKAN, Dataverse and GeoNode paths on about 30 hosts; datos.gob.mx, the Paraguayan GeoNode, Uruguay's CKAN) left no recorded value in sections 5 and 6 and were not re-read.
- First-pass robots slips reported by the agents; under the owner's rule of 12 Nov they are no longer slips (several of those sources are now cited again, see above): can-a (CKAN API calls on catalogue.data.gov.bc.ca and open.alberta.ca), mex-a and mex-b (datos.gob.mx CKAN, IDEGeo API), sa-cone (GeoNode /api/v2 on geoportal.mades.gov.py and a CKAN API call on catalogodatos.gub.uy), br-1 (paisagenslidar.cnptia.embrapa.br, deleted), global (an IOP article, void), carib-e (one robots-disallowed path on bvi.gov.vg). Second-pass slips: r2-mex2 made two early requests (Wayback availability API) with curl outside the helper; r2-mex1 and r2-rem each created an empty file in /tmp by mistake and deleted it; r2-rem fetched the Dataforsyningen product record and FAQ through the site's own `/es/` search endpoints (the site's robots.txt disallows nothing). The lead probed six candidate `/metadata/` paths on dataforsyningen.dk with HEAD (all 404) and read mapterhorn's Greenland source notes on api.github.com (one directory listing) and raw.githubusercontent.com (three files), both robots-checked and neither a mapterhorn.com host.
- r2-cacov fetched the Natural Earth GeoJSON from raw.githubusercontent.com (no robots rules) after github.com's `/*/raw/` rule refused the geoBoundaries links, and a 133-byte Git LFS pointer; if raw.githubusercontent.com counts as part of the github.com block, discard the boundary source (the Canada shares then need other province polygons). Under the owner's rule of 12 Nov this boundary source stands (re-fetched on 12 Nov for the Alberta spatial query, 2,325,694 bytes).
- Crawl delays respected: opentopography.org 10 s, open.canada.ca 20 s, ign.gob.ar 10 s, nsidc.org 5 s, data.bris.ac.uk 30 s, metadados.inde.gov.br and metadadosgeo.ibge.gov.br 30 s, ArcGIS Hub hosts 60 s, geohub-gnl 60 s, datos.gob.mx and several CKAN portals 10 s. mapterhorn.com's robots.txt names AI crawlers (including ClaudeBot, anthropic-ai, Claude-Web) and sets `ai-train=no`; the first pass made about 15 small requests to it with our own User-Agent (not one of the names) and the second pass none; the owner has asked that it not be fetched again, and its entries here are cited from the first-pass reads.
- Sizes: no data files were downloaded. The Bermuda 308 MB geodatabase, the Quebec 1 m MNT index zip and several PDFs (INEGI, MDE-Ar, Asiaq GK24) were fetched whole and read; a few large answers (IGN WFS layers, INDE GeoServer capabilities, a Nunavut polygon, a 9 MB ANADEM STAC item, Bahia's 12.3 MB plan) were not read whole. `head` requests are not logged. The Canada recomputation fetched 205 small S3 extent files (13.7 MB in total, largest 996 KB) and one 2.3 MB coverage file.
- Harness: several sub-agents' attempts to write a FINDINGS.md were refused ("Subagents should return findings as text, not write report files"); they returned their findings as text instead. The permission layer also denied a pip install (sa-north) and a raw multi-host curl loop (br-1).
- Data quality notes: several first-pass OpenTopography landing-page answers were soft-error pages saved with HTTP 200 and are not cited; a duplicated log id occurs in a few folders (background loops overlapped). One first-pass agent's shell `grep` was a broken function and hid a text hit until rechecked.
- Lead verification: all URLs cited in sections 2-4 were matched against the fetch logs (script in the scratch folder, run last on the final text). About 25 first-pass claims and about 20 second-pass claims were checked against saved answers (Uruguay, Ecuador, Santa Catarina, Pernambuco, INEGI, Canada licences, Copernicus and DLR licences, ASF, Argentina IGN FAQ, St Vincent, Bonaire, Quebec, Asiaq, Mapterhorn, plus the second-pass Cordoba GetCoverage, Nicaragua contours, Yukon file server, Manitoba MLI, Saint Lucia, Cayman, BVI Anegada, Greenland GHM, SPM download service). The coverage computations were not recomputed except Canada (done by a separate agent from allowed sources and compared with the first pass).
- Scratch: at the end of the second pass the scratch folder `terr-americas/` was emptied except the `fetch.log` files (one per agent folder, including lead/ and the second-pass folders), which are the record of every URL fetched.

## 7. URLs read

4,239 unique URLs returned HTTP 2xx or 206 across the 15 first-pass agents (2,454), the 12 second-pass agents and the lead (robots.txt requests excluded). Every URL printed in this document is among them (checked against the logs by script on the final text), except the URLs read on 12 Nov and listed after the table; the per-request records are the `fetch.log` files kept in the scratch folder `terr-americas/`, one per agent folder (columns: id, UTC time, HTTP status, bytes, content type or `robots`, URL, saved file, kind). The logs also hold the first-pass reads that the superseded robots audit had removed (section 6); the facts restored from them were re-read on 12 Nov unless marked otherwise. "Requests logged" counts every logged row, including robots.txt, redirects and errors.

URLs read for the first time on 12 Nov (the re-read after the owner's robots rule; same User-Agent, one request every one to two seconds), not in the fetch logs: the Ontario image-service group-by queries on `VerticalAccuracyClass` and `AcqMethod` (same base URL as the logged `Project` query, Category=1); the Quebec WFS sample pages with `propertyName=DENSITE,PROJET,TYPE_DONNEE,SYSREF_ALTIMETRIQUE` at `startIndex` 0, 100000, 200000, 300000, 400000, 500000 and 600000 (300 tiles each); a range read of `https://diffusion.mern.gouv.qc.ca/diffusion/RGQ/Lidar/2022_Centrequebecchaudiereappalaches_Lidar_Den10_DonneesClassifiees/Mtm7/Laz/22_2035123F07_DC.laz`; a POST to the HRDEM Project Footprints layer 5 `query` with the Natural Earth Alberta polygon (spatial relation intersects, fields provider and project_name); the Panama API search `q=lidar&rows=10`; the 145 Redape dataset records `https://www.redape.dados.embrapa.br/api/datasets/:persistentId/?persistentId=doi:10.48432/<id>` for the DOIs of the "LiDAR survey" search hits (the ORDAWS record is also in the logs).
